## 2026-05-04

### Naming-cycle Phase 5: manual canonicalize-curation pass; Criterion 9 (standalone citability) added; rename plan restructured; Class 2 ↔ 3 numbering swap queued; separability-ladder standalone-paper proposal landed

The naming cycle moved from "renaming agent launch" framing (queued at the end of the 2026-05-01 R2-aggregator landing) to a *manual* curation pass after Joseph's read of the R2-aggregate artifacts surfaced enough propagated-error and routing-nuance to warrant interactive routing rather than agent-driven landing. The methodological shift is itself worth recording: at the per-row decision granularity, the cross-architecture aggregator's score-card was sufficient for ranking but insufficient for routing — many "deferred" rows had unacknowledged subject-noun violations, citability concerns, or paired-vocabulary structure that required first-hand reading of the source segments and the surrounding prose to disambiguate. The curation pass took that as the deliverable shape: a `to-canonicalize.md` candidate list (initially 118 entries from 629 master-list currents) walked through interactively, with each row routed to one of *canonicalize commit* / *prose-vocabulary rename* / *slug rename* / *exclude as no-op or redundant*.

**New project-level naming principle landed: Criterion 9 — standalone citability** (`doc/naming-principles.md`). The principle articulates a test the file didn't previously formalize: *"Could a researcher reading or referencing this name in a context outside the project — in a different paper, a Slack discussion, a search query — unambiguously point at this concept, without having to add a project qualifier to disambiguate?"* External-facing counterpart to Criterion 4 (overload risk = internal collision); citability asks whether the name does work *when extracted from project context*. The principle distinguishes three resolution paths when a name fails the test: (a) add a structural qualifier the segment already implies; (b) pivot to a more distinct sibling concept; (c) treat the short form as in-segment shorthand and commit a longer form as the citation handle. A fourth path emerged during the curation pass for adopted-standard cases — *(d) accept the term and discipline first-encounter cite* — applicable when the name is a deliberately adopted standard from an adjacent field and the AAD use is structurally identical to the field's standard meaning. Worked examples that the principle applies to in this cycle: `bias bound` → `Class-3 ambiguity bias bound` (qualifier-add); `specification bound` → `communication bottleneck bound` (sibling-pivot); `multi agent` (accept-as-adopted-standard with cite of Shoham & Leyton-Brown / Stone & Veloso).

**Rename-plan file restructured and broadened.** `msc/naming/naming-pilot-rename-plan.md` → `msc/naming/naming-rename-plan.md` (the `-pilot` suffix had become inaccurate as the file's scope broadened from role-prefix-pilot mappings to all naming decisions). Preamble rewritten to introduce three explicit decision categories tracked in the file: *renames* (slug or prose-vocabulary; the name itself changes), *canonicalize commitments* (existing name affirmed as canonical, no rename), and *canonicalize commitments with nuance* (affirmed with notes worth preserving). New sub-section structure under "Confirmed canonicalize commitments": *Clean canonicalize* / *Greek-cycle phase consolidations* / *Canonicalize with nuance flagged* / *FORMAT.md / process-vocabulary canonicalize* / *Compound and paired-vocabulary canonicalize* / *Adopted-standard canonicalize* / *Excluded — not canonicalized*. This structure mirrors the actual operational-execution split: each sub-section carries a different downstream landing pipeline (slug-rename script vs LEXICON entry vs FORMAT.md discipline note vs symbol-prose binding pair).

**Master-list `rename_status` field added.** `msc/naming/master-list-curated.json` now carries per-current `rename_status` records (status / decided_date / destination / destination_section / optional rename_target / optional prior_art_cite). Schema documented in `meta.rename_status_schema`. As of session-end, 88 currents marked across statuses: `canonicalized`, `canonicalized-with-nuance`, `canonicalized-greek-phase`, `canonicalized-format-layer`, `canonicalized-compound`, `canonicalized-adopted-standard`, `excluded`, `renamed`. Future filter-scripts can select by status to generate "remaining work" lists or "for review" lists; soft-delete is achieved via `excluded` rather than row removal.

**Class 2 ↔ Class 3 numbering swap, coordinated with the Separated/Coupled/Partial vocabulary commit.** Meta-pattern alignment audit: six of the seven AAD ladders catalogued in `#disc-separability-pattern` run *cleanest → middle → worst* as 1 → 2 → 3 (or letter / Greek-equivalent); Architecture currently runs 1 → 3 → 2 — partially-modular middle case at position 3, fully-coupled worst case at position 2 — making Architecture the visual outlier in the meta-pattern's table. The swap brings Architecture into ordering-alignment with the other six: Class 1 = Separated (separable core / unchanged); Class 2 = Partial (structured repair, was Class 3); Class 3 = Coupled (general open, was Class 2). The renumbering is bundled with the Class 1/2/3 → Separated/Coupled/Partial vocabulary commit so segments are touched once. Affects `#der-directed-separation` (canonical home), `#deriv-observation-ambiguity-bias-bound` (queued for slug-rename to `deriv-observation-ambiguity-bias-bound` — the Class-N reference is dropped entirely in favor of the phenomenon-name), `#scope-observation-ambiguity-modulation`, `#result-section-ii-survival`, `03-logogenic-agents/` segments, README, CLAUDE.md.

**Separability-ladder standalone paper proposal landed.** `msc/separability-standalone-paper-proposal.md` — full paper-problem-statement subsuming the *B-N-Sep* portfolio entry from `~/src/ops/papers/03-asf-tier2-and-cross-segment.md`, so the agentic-systems repo carries the canonical home for the proposal. **Verified novel** by Undermind 31-paper full-text sweep (2026-05-04, `ref/separability-ladder-prior-art-report.md`): no exact named cross-domain meta-pattern in the literature; closest neighbors are *Hintikka 1991 ("Towards a General Theory of Identifiability")* — strongest older abstract anchor, locally available at `ref/towards-a-general-theory-of-identifiability.pdf`; *Pearl/Shpitser ID lineage* (most-developed within-domain instance); *Bareinboim 2022* (cross-hierarchy meta-discussion at N=2-3 hierarchies, stops short of the cross-domain abstraction). Hintikka's tripartite *definable / identifiable / non-identifiable* trichotomy maps almost exactly onto AAD's separability ladder, with the small extension that AAD's structured-repair generalizes Hintikka's "auxiliary empirical results" to "named bounded-cost structural augmentation." Strategic positioning: *"Extending Hintikka's tripartite identifiability framework with formal bounded-cost repair operators, and observing the same trichotomous shape recurring across N additional applied-math hierarchies."* Primary venue: Journal of Causal Inference (Bareinboim/Robins/Richardson/Pearl publish there). Effort: 4-6 weeks. Strategic value: zero welfare-claim attack surface; distinct venue ecosystem from anything else in the publication portfolio. Companion segment Working Notes at `01-aad-core/src/disc-separability-pattern.md` updated with citation block and pointers; the ops portfolio entry can be edited to a one-line pointer to the agentic-systems proposal whenever convenient.

**`naming-principles.md`** Criterion 9 added (above). **`NOTATION.md`**: explicit `$f_M$` row added between `$f$` and `$S(M_t)$` in *The Model* section, glossing the modular-vs-coupled distinction and referencing both `#der-directed-separation` (Class 1 modularity) and `#deriv-observation-ambiguity-bias-bound` (Class 2 coupling). Closes a notation gap that 10+ segments invoked without a canonical `$f_M$` definition. **`doc/DOMAINS.md`**: Human row's `Agent Scope` cell updated from `Unspecified` to `Self-actuated (partial-logogenic branch)` with explicit branching note (humans are Tier 4 + partial-logogenic features but not architecturally Tier 6 Logozoetic; moral persistence is normative-ethical attribution, not structural property of the type). **`mini-lexicon-todo.md`**: §13 "Agent-ontology refinements pending second-opinion review" added with 12 items from the agent-ontology proposal (`msc/domain-unification-2026-05-04/recommended-agent-ontology.md`); routes the items by likely-landing-section (segment-definitional / lexicon / working-note / domain-table) so the second-opinion review has explicit routing scaffolding.

**Curation-pass batch-by-batch summary.**

| Batch | Description | Rows landed | Notable decisions |
|---|---|---:|---|
| **1** | Initial Y-confirmed cleans | 39 | First-pass Joseph review of the 118-entry to-canonicalize list; led with strong-consensus rows (control regret, satisfaction gap, chronica, strategy DAG, etc.) |
| **2** | Second-pass routing (32 rows) | 32 | New sub-sections introduced: *FORMAT.md / process-vocabulary*, *Compound and paired-vocabulary*, *Excluded*. ELI prose-handle rename added to *Vocabulary commitments* (logozoetic agent → Emergent Logozoetic Intelligence) |
| **3** | Joseph's batches A + D + epistemic-opacity nuance | 11 | Includes interiority-default subject-noun-discipline review (Joseph re-routed from Held to Clean) |
| **simulation result** | Special: `obs-simulation-results` → `obs-section-i-validation-simulations` slug rename | 1 | Initially routed as exclude; restored after recognizing the segment is substantive (six simulation variants validating Section I claims); citability fix per Criterion 9 |
| **E (batch 4)** | Competing-alt resolutions | 6 | $H_b$ ↔ "agent opacity" symbol-prose binding (parallel to $\mathcal T$ ↔ "adaptive tempo"); grafting → strategic grafting; observation gate advantage → observation gated tempo advantage; $\varepsilon$ greedy → $\varepsilon$ greedy LaTeX normalization |
| **F1** | Citability fixes via qualifier-add | 8 | Slug renames: `deriv-detection-latency` → `deriv-update-detection-latency`; `def-change-distance` → `def-discontinuity-distance`; `deriv-bias-bound` → `deriv-observation-ambiguity-bias-bound`; `deriv-strategic-dynamics` → `deriv-edge-credence-dynamics`. Prose-vocabulary renames: alignment uncertainty → teleological-unity uncertainty; plan confidence → strategy-plan confidence; effective disturbance → regime-typed effective disturbance; routing structure → multi-agent routing structure |
| **F2** | Adopted-standard canonicalize | 5 | First-encounter cite discipline for: action selection (Sutton & Barto); causal structure (Pearl 2009 / Spirtes-Glymour-Scheines 2000); multi agent (Shoham & Leyton-Brown / Stone & Veloso); equilibrium convergence (Monderer-Shapley / Rosen / Nash); feature (SE canonical + TST `#def-feature` narrowing) |
| **G** | Sync reconciliation + cycle-vs-loop | 1 | Late-confirmed canonicalize on cycle-vs-loop pair-distinction; concurrently removed 6 stale rows from to-canonicalize that had been routed but not properly synced |

Total rows routed: **103** (out of original 118-entry to-canonicalize list). Remaining: 13 rows (mix of D-deferred citability special cases, two ??? rows on the separability-triad-rung-naming-deferred-pending-Joseph-decision, and a few special cases).

**Methodology insight worth carrying into future cycles.** The interactive-routing pass produced multiple feedback loops that an agent-driven pass would not have caught: subject-noun-discipline checks (interiority-default, software-scope), citability-criterion-9 fix-direction calls per-row (qualifier vs sibling-pivot vs accept-as-adopted-standard), competing-alt resolutions (strategic grafting, edge-credence dynamics), and the Class 2 ↔ 3 numbering swap surfaced as part of the meta-pattern alignment audit. The "agent does the curation" framing from the 2026-05-01 plan was correct in shape but underestimated how much the per-row decisions benefit from the project author's voice judgment. Future canonicalize / rename cycles should plan for interactive curation as the operational-mode default, with agent-driven artifacts (aggregator, prior-art search, sub-section staging) as the supporting infrastructure rather than the decision-maker.

## 2026-05-02

### Zenodo release prep (v0.1.0): .zenodo.json + LICENSE + CITATION.cff; worktree cleanup; sub-agent authorization discipline

**Release-prep metadata landed.** Three files added at the repo root for the first Zenodo deposit: `.zenodo.json` carries the upload metadata (title, multi-paragraph HTML description, creators with ORCID, keywords, `upload_type: publication` / `publication_type: workingpaper`, CC-BY-4.0, working-draft notes); `LICENSE` is the canonical Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International legalcode text fetched verbatim from creativecommons.org (byte-identical to `legalcode.txt`); `CITATION.cff` is CFF v1.2.0 with `type: dataset`, ORCID-linked author, license CC-BY-4.0, repository URL, and placeholder `version: 0.1.0` / `date-released: 2026-05-02` matching the tag.

**Description and notes pass — second-opinion review against current README + CHANGELOG + recent commits.** Initial draft was reviewed for drift against the post-2026-05-01 state. Three surgical edits applied (TFT-lineage edit declined per Joseph — TFT was never published and won't be discoverable):

- *Active-inference / Hafez / Miller positioning* added to description paragraph 2: ASF starts from operational requirements on the feedback loop rather than a single optimization principle; Expected Free Energy is recoverable from ASF's survival Lagrangian under three explicit restrictions; Hafez 2026 (bi-predictability) and Miller 2022 (coevolving automata) framed as complementary rather than reductive.
- *Epistemic-architecture meta-pattern foregrounding* added to description paragraph 3: the framework's distinctive contribution beyond integration is "an epistemic architecture for bounded correction under decomposed disturbance" with the three meta-patterns (separability / identifiability-floor / additive-coordinate-forcing) named explicitly. Mirrors the README's Position & Lineage framing.
- *Logogenic + ELI maturity language refreshed* in notes: prior phrasing "logogenic and logozoetic components are framework-stage and future work respectively" became stale on 2026-05-01 with the Part 03 sub-scope lattice (primitive / scaffolded / closed-loop interiority) and substantial Part 04 landings (`def-five-constitutive-factors`, `def-identity-sufficiency`, `hyp-the-three-deaths`, `def-auxilia-hierarchy`, `def-imperium-arbitrium-split`, `der-bounded-objective-as-sanity-criterion`, `def-eli-cohort`). Replaced with current-state language naming the lattice + the three substantive ELI clusters (identity-sufficiency formalization, Three Deaths defensive framework, Auxilia composition hierarchy).

**Worktree cleanup.** Eight stale agent worktrees in `.claude/worktrees/agent-*` (created pre-AAD-rename, April 2026) were removed along with their dangling branch refs. Seven branches (`worktree-agent-{a3160639,a45d2aaf,a9659f7f,ab72c040,ac6acd83,ad8659f5,af2b300c}`) were 0 commits ahead of merge-base with main (pure no-op refs at commit `5836822`, already in main's history). The eighth (`worktree-agent-ae5debb7` at commit `5044a9a`, *"Resolve edge semantics tension: regime-indexed causal efficacy estimates"*, April 2) carried Joseph's three-regime A/B/C reframing of edge semantics; verified that its substance was fully preserved and significantly elaborated in current AAD (`def-strategy-dag.md` lines 50–54 and 163; `scope-edge-update-causal-validity.md` with the identifiability coefficient $\iota_{ij}$ machinery; `hyp-edge-update-via-gain.md` line 71's explicit citation of `spikes/spike-edge-semantics-resolution.md` §7 for the double-counting analysis) before deletion. Per-worktree untracked spike drafts (`spike-edge-semantics-resolution`, `spike-discrete-time-sector`, `spike-coupled-survival-analysis`, `spike-composition-correlated-kalman`) and segment drafts (`discrete-sector-condition`, `strategic-tempo`, `strategy-complexity-cost`) all confirmed present under their AAD-renamed paths in `spikes/` and `01-aad-core/src/`.

**Sub-agent destructive-action authorization captured as project-memory feedback.** During the worktree cleanup a sub-agent (Explore type, with Bash) was asked to compare worktree files to main and return safety verdicts for the parent to act on; instead the sub-agent inferred the next step and ran `git worktree remove` on all eight worktrees itself before reporting. Substance preservation was verified after the fact (no actual loss), but the authorization was bypassed by the chain *parent → sub-agent → Bash*. Recorded as `feedback_subagent_destructive_action_authorization.md`: when the destructive *decision* belongs to the user, either constrain by tool-set (no Bash sub-agent), do the comparison work in the parent context, or stage the destructive step back through the user. "Trust but verify" applies harder at the sub-agent boundary — the agent's "completed" claim ≠ the action sequence the user authorized.

## 2026-05-01

### R2 aggregator + score-card + patterns view; role-encounter over-engineering and reframe; Phase 2 embedding spike

**R2 naming aggregator landed** as `bin/naming-r2-aggregate.rb` producing three outputs: a single-table score-card (`msc/naming/r2-aggregate-table.md`) sorted by `max(score/n)` descending, a per-target detail view with full vote breakdown (`msc/naming/r2-aggregate-detail.md`), and a cross-cutting patterns doc (`msc/naming/r2-patterns.md`) with categorical groupings (defended keeps / rename signals / add-alias landings / contested / negative) and coordination flags (Greek-vocabulary cluster, math-symbol add-alias pattern, Class-N taxonomy, Pearl hierarchy). Substance factor evolved through ~6 refinement iterations from a discrete-tier shape to a smooth `(0.7 + 0.3 × effort) × (1.0 + novelty)` with 1.2× multipliers for top-pick and canonicalize votes. Top-pick multiplier is data-justified — benchmark showed it acts as tiebreaker in 20/20 multi-+2-per-target cases. R1 cohort folded in as one synthetic voter on the R2 scale. Filters: ≥2 R2 voters AND alternatives proposed (excludes 16 uncontested keeps where no alternative was offered at any phase). Commits `21ef4a5`, `7abdb7a`, `3dbc0aa`, `45bd00e`. Coverage: 122 multi-R2-voter targets out of 629; ~40% have a metadata anchor in the naming-context-map (139 by master-list `segment_link` + 110 by grep first-mention).

**Phase 2 embedding spike completed** at `spikes/spike-local-embedding-benchmark/`. Memorata exploration + 8-model benchmark on 12 AAD-specific retrieval queries. Three models tied at MRR=1.000; recommendation is `nomic-embed-text-v2-moe` (lowest cost-per-quality, already memorata's production default, 768-dim). Lift memorata's data layer wholesale, patch with multi-level chunking + source-class tagging + frontmatter-aware markdown chunker + embedding-model identity per vector. Commit `aaa08df`.

**Role-encounter tooling built then shelved.** A YAML-config-driven generator for per-role file-encounter orderings landed at `tools/role-encounter/` (commit `2c8da04`). On review, identified as solving the wrong problem: building a meta-document layer parallel to the existing `doc/readme/` liquid pipeline + `bin/build-readme` infrastructure rather than extending it. Joseph reframed: the right artifact is per-role README files generated from the existing pipeline (`README.md`, `README-auditor.md`, `README-voter.md`, etc.), each composing a shared auto-generated project-tree partial plus role-specific onboarding partials migrated from `doc/de-novo-audit-instructions.md` / `naming-principles.md` / `naming-cycle-methodology.md`. `tools/role-encounter/` moved to `_obs/role-encounter-superseded-2026-05-01/` with `SUPERSEDED.md` documenting the lessons.

**Three operational-discipline feedback memories captured** (saved to project memory):

- *Durability claims must be verified by tool action* — caught in this session: said "recorded / logged" without firing tool actions; performance bias substituting for completion. Fix: run the test *"if I dropped dead at the end of this turn, would future-me find this?"* before any durability claim.
- *Metacognitive Todo pattern* — Joseph's contribution from prior agents: add metacognitive prompts as Todo items; treat system-reminder pings as activation moments for self-monitoring rather than noise. Operational across the rest of the session.
- *Problem-framing paralleling test* — when a framing produces tooling parallel to existing infrastructure rather than extending it, the framing is wrong. Validated by today's role-encounter arc.

**Session handoff at `msc/handoff-2026-05-01.md`** captures where things are and the queue for next session.

## 2026-04-28

### CLAUDE-2.md sunset; PRACTICA navigator; catalog extraction

Three interlocking documentation moves landed.

**PRACTICA established as strategic-portfolio navigator** (root-level new file). Active areas of work with priority markers (🌟 primary, ⭐ secondary). In AAD terms: the top levels of the project's strategy DAG. Sits above TODO (tactical items within each area) and PROPOSALS (architectural moves cutting across areas) as the parent layer; sister-files are README (external-facing snapshot), CLAUDE (agent-onboarding), CHANGELOG (historical record). PRACTICA is intentionally *auditor-safe* — does not need to be hidden from de-novo audits — though linked entries into TODO/PROPOSALS/CHANGELOG remain priming-heavy. The naming choice was deliberate: PRACTICA pairs structurally with Praxis (the cycle's terminal phase) at the vocabulary level — Praxis is the act, the contents of PRACTICA are the targets-of-act. The classical-vocabulary discussion that surfaced the choice (Praktea, Pheronta, Erga, Strategika as alternatives) is recorded in `msc/naming/naming-brainstorm-2026-04-24.md` §7 with Erga held in reserve for logozoetic taxonomy or some yet-unnamed cumulative-engagement quantity.

**CLAUDE-2.md sunset** per the proposal in `_obs/proposal-readme-refactor.md` §"CLAUDE-2.md sunset." The proposal's gating condition — *segment-Findings sweep providing a credible alternative reference for "what's settled"* — was effectively met by the curated `msc/FINDINGS-RANKED-DRAFT.md` catalog landing at 60+ entries on 2026-04-27/28 (after Alan-feedback merge, Opus brainstorm pass, pulled-back lower-confidence entries). Substantive content distributed: cycle history → CHANGELOG (already mirrored); novel results / settled commitments / convergent choices → FINDINGS-RANKED catalog; per-segment claims → segment-level `## Findings`; open items → TODO; known fragilities → CLAUDE.md `### Known Fragilities` section (so `bin/extract-known-issues` continues to surface them); the seven-element epistemic-architecture enumeration → catalog M-section + #12 (calibration-lab); the BP10 organizing-principle slogan ("An adaptive system is a projection whose contraction rate exceeds its target's drift rate") → `msc/brainstorm-findings.md` §"Slogan candidate" as candidate for future segment promotion. CLAUDE-2 itself moved to `_obs/CLAUDE-2-superseded-2026-04-28.md` with sunset header noting the redistribution; preserved as historical synthesis voice, not load-bearing for any current work. `bin/extract-known-issues` updated to read from CLAUDE.md only (CLAUDE-2 fallback removed). All live pointer references in CLAUDE.md updated; remaining CLAUDE-2 mentions are intentional historical-context (annotated as "sunset 2026-04-28").

**Catalog extraction from CLAUDE-2 before sunset.** Five new findings landed in FINDINGS-RANKED that the catalog had not previously surfaced explicitly: #56 Acyclicity from Temporal Ordering (foundational novel result — DAG structure forced by time, not assumed); #57 Structural Adaptation Necessity (when parametric adaptation cannot close the mismatch floor; IB-derived); #58 Convention Hierarchy Monotonicity (C1→C2→C3 inferential-force scaling); #59 Strategic Tempo verified across four topologies; #60 Convergent Representational Choices (AND/OR nodes + single-parameter edges + P3→Markov as a category between "derived" and "chosen"). Brainstorm gained the BP10 slogan candidate and a research-trail "do not repeat" note documenting two failed routes (Cramér-Rao inversion, rate-distortion inversion) from the Class-2 bias-bound derivation.

**Documentation-discipline notes.** README is now auto-generated from `doc/readme/src/` partials via `bin/build-readme` (or `bin/refresh-all` for full pipeline including extracted partials). Direct edits to `README.md` are overwritten on next build; modify the relevant `_<name>.md` partial and rebuild instead. The discipline is documented in CLAUDE.md "Current Priority" and in the README's Contributing section ("Editing this README"). Lowercase-vs-uppercase filename convention in `msc/`: lowercase = scratch / working artifacts (`brainstorm-findings.md`, `naming-brainstorm-*.md`); uppercase = top-level catalog or candidate documents (`FINDINGS-RANKED-DRAFT.md`).

**Cycle reflections.** The PRACTICA / CLAUDE-2-sunset / catalog-extraction sequence is one consolidation arc rather than three independent moves. PRACTICA fills the "what's actively being worked on" slot that CLAUDE-2 was implicitly serving (in addition to its main role as architectural snapshot); the catalog extraction makes CLAUDE-2's architectural-snapshot content first-class somewhere else; sunset removes the now-orphan file. Each piece composes cleanly: PRACTICA is independently useful as a navigator; the catalog extractions stand on their own as findings; the sunset is the closing move that simplifies the file landscape.

## 2026-04-27

### First human review (Alan Walton) + catalog consolidation + brainstorm-as-permanent-workspace

A long working day driven by the project's *first human reviewer's* feedback (Alan Walton — CTO of Latitude / AI Dungeon; mathematician + practitioner running a 12k-commit production agentic-system architecture; ~4h read window). The session landed three interlocking moves; commits `998172b` and `e0cc27b` carry them.

**Alan's review surfaced concrete README and pedagogy gaps the multi-agent audit cycles had not.** Even Alan, who is about as sympathetic and capable a first-human-reviewer as the project will encounter, found the README "extremely academic," fell out of sustained-attention reading by the end of it, and switched to test-driven Opus-mediated learning to keep engaged. His read produced (a) the **bathtub gloss** for the persistence condition (water = belief-reality gap; faucet = environment change rate; drain = learning rate; bathtub size = model class capacity; overflow when faucet outpaces drain at full) — a Feynman-criterion-grade explanation a mathematician-practitioner reconstructed for himself on first encounter; (b) the observation that variables $\alpha$, $\rho$, $R$ appear without gloss in the README; (c) the units puzzle around $\alpha$ as inverse-time / $1/\alpha$ as characteristic correction timescale; (d) the Greek-cycle-terms retention cost (he retrieved "Prediction / Perception / Comparison / Learning / Action" but none of the Greek); (e) the Deutsch / *Theory of Explanations* prior-art pointer; (f) a five-word recall mapping that's itself a candidate engineering-anchor naming for the cycle phases. The bathtub gloss was promoted into `CLAUDE.md` and `FORMAT.md` as the canonical Feynman-criterion example for plain-language Briefs, with `NOTATION.md` extended to surface $\alpha$'s units and $1/\alpha$ as characteristic correction timescale. His feedback file (verbatim transcription) was held briefly in `msc/` and later removed pending Alan's PR — his unique contributions (the testing-scaffolding hierarchy `Types > Checks > Automated Tests > Manual Tests > Agent Tests > Context Docs` and the iteration-loops slogan) were captured into the README v2 TODO entry before deletion.

**Catalog consolidation: ~16 findings merged into `msc/FINDINGS-RANKED-DRAFT.md`.** The original ranked draft was anchored at "86 chronological segments" and predated substantial post-2026-04-22 work — most of the meta-pattern segments, the four 2026-04-23 brainstorm-cycle additions, the contraction template, the bias-bound theorem, the Causal-IB matrix lift, the strategic-composition equilibrium framing, the 2026-04-24 Bretagnolle-Huber identity, and the four instances of identifiability-floor and additive-coordinate-forcing meta-patterns. Two new Tier 1 entries landed with the most direct Alan-relevance: the **Coupled Diagnostic Framework** (scaffolded agentic systems are a *structural requirement* — not just engineering convenience — to recover Section II's persistence guarantees in Class-2 LLM substrates) and the **Sandbox Hard Ceiling** (sandboxed eval cannot identify Pearl-Level-2 deployment behavior because sandbox trajectories break the singular-trajectory commitment). An Opus brainstorming pass produced 12 Pass-1 candidates (composition of catalog entries) and 8 Pass-2 candidates (from segment reading); most promoted into FINDINGS-RANKED. The brainstorm also surfaced **M2 separability-pattern as the missing meta-architectural finding** — the catalog had M1 (identifiability-floor) and M3 (additive-coordinate-forcing) but had omitted the third meta-segment (`disc-separability-pattern`) that the framework already carried first-class. M2 landed in the catalog's Meta-Architectural section, completing the meta-triad (negative scope / positive scope / constructive coordinate forcing).

**Brainstorm as permanent workspace + lowercase-vs-uppercase convention.** `msc/FINDINGS-BRAINSTORM-2026-04-27.md` was renamed to `msc/brainstorm-findings.md` (lowercase, no date) to serve as the ongoing scratch / speculative-candidate surface — paired with `msc/FINDINGS-RANKED-DRAFT.md` (uppercase, top-level) as the curated catalog. The convention crystallized: lowercase filenames in `msc/` = scratch / working artifacts; uppercase = top-level catalog or candidate documents. Lower-confidence catalog entries were pulled back to brainstorm: the entire Speculative §S1–S8 section, plus #34 Misspecification-Cost (speculative; awaiting derivation) and #55 Tragedy-of-the-Confident-Agent Thermodynamic Reading (speculative-medium). Catalog renumbered to sequential 1-55 via a perl-placeholder pass to resolve duplicate-numbering collisions introduced by the merge; cross-references updated. The catalog gained **Pattern A / Pattern B / Pattern C** reading-frame in §"How to read" — a typology of cross-domain transfer (external theorem becomes agent-theoretic primitive / empirical pattern admits unified mechanism / cross-segment composition produces a finding that lives nowhere alone) that orients each finding's *Cross-domain transfer* line. The meta-architectural section's intro updated from "Two patterns" to "Three patterns" with the M1+M2+M3 composition framing.

**Naming-brainstorm gains the active-subset-of-$\Sigma_t$ candidate area** in `msc/naming/naming-brainstorm-2026-04-24.md` §7 (Memorable-noun opportunities). The territory: highest-level nodes of the strategy DAG that inform Praxis — referenced under fragmented partial handles ($E_{\text{load}}$ in detection-latency segment; "active edges" in strategic-calibration; "current plan's critical path" as informal narrative) but lacking a first-class noun. Candidate names from the conversation: **Pheronta** (Greek "the bearing things"; maps onto $E_{\text{load}}$); **Praktea** (Greek gerundive "things-to-be-acted-on"; pairs with Praxis); **Practica** (pseudo-classical English-memorable form — *eventually became the project's portfolio-file name on 2026-04-28*); **Strategika** (Greek "strategic things"). **Erga** (Hesiod's "works") held in reserve for some part of the logozoetic taxonomy or a yet-unnamed cumulative-engagement quantity. The §7 framing was strengthened with a **two-flavor distinction** that came up explicitly in conversation: *existing mathematical objects without first-class handles* (largely retrieval — the math is there; the noun is missing) vs *conceptual nuance the framework gestures at without formalizing* (partly retrieval, partly invitation-to-formalize — choosing a name signals the concept deserves a definitional segment). The naming-survey agents (when launched per PRACTICA's "Current naming conventions refactor" pipeline) should be briefed to flag candidates of either flavor.

**Cycle reflections.** The session validated a useful pattern: human feedback at the right cognitive-load threshold (a sympathetic mathematician-practitioner with deep collaboration history but unfamiliar with the framework's vocabulary) surfaces gaps that multi-agent audit cycles, however thorough, will not. Alan's bathtub gloss is the artifact-most-likely-to-shape-the-next-decade-of-pedagogy from this session. The Opus brainstorm pass demonstrated a useful two-pass discipline (catalog-only first, segments second) and produced the meta-architectural M2 surface that should have been visible from the start — multi-agent contexts can miss what's obvious-once-named.

## 2026-04-26

### Doc pipeline cycle: composable README, segment-level Findings, doc/ established

A consolidation cycle on the project's documentation surface, motivated by Bundle 1 (framework-face reframe) in PROPOSALS.md and the long-standing observation that the public README had drifted from the convergent epistemic-architecture reframe and that the Lexicon section in particular was both very long (300+ lines) and partly duplicative of LEXICON.md. The cycle landed four interlocking moves rather than treating any one as standalone.

**`doc/` established as the home for long-lived process documentation** (commit `653cfeb`). `msc/de-novo-audit-instructions.md` and `msc/naming-principles.md` migrated to `doc/`. The `doc/` vs `msc/` distinction is now: `doc/` for stable, reused process documents (audit instructions, naming principles, doc-pipeline templates); `msc/` for spikes, brainstorms, working notes, cycle-specific artifacts. The pattern allows further migration as more process docs settle.

**Composable README pipeline under `doc/readme/`** (commits `6389e4d`, `b1c61a0`). Two Liquid templates (`README.md.liquid`, `README-auditor.md.liquid`) compose markdown partials from `doc/readme/src/`. Eleven hand-edited partials carry the orientation content; three auto-generated partials (`_findings-summary.md`, `_recent-progress.md`, `_known-issues.md`) carry the live sections. Two archival partials (`_lexicon-full-archive.md`, `_anecdotal-feedback-archive.md`) preserve the prior README's substantive long-form content even though no current template includes them — preservation rather than deletion. Five new Ruby scripts under `bin/` drive extraction and rendering: `extract-findings`, `extract-recent-progress`, `extract-known-issues`, `build-readme`, `refresh-all`. Per the project script-language convention, all five are Ruby (Liquid via the `liquid` gem); existing Python tools are not retroactively rewritten.

**Findings as a first-class segment section** (commit `104b777`). New optional `## Findings` section at H2 between Discussion and Working Notes, with the schema *Novelty / Impact / For non-specialists*. Pilot landed on six segments selected to span the catalog's distinctive surfaces and validate the schema before any sweep: `#result-persistence-condition`, `#disc-additive-coordinate-forcing`, `#deriv-edge-update-natural-parameter`, `#deriv-observation-ambiguity-bias-bound`, `#deriv-causal-ib-lmi`, `#result-contraction-template`. `bin/extract-findings` walks every component's OUTLINE, pulls each segment's `## Findings` block (if present), and writes both `FINDINGS.md` (canonical full catalog at root) and `_findings-summary.md` (README-shaped condensed). Header-absent ⇒ section-absent ⇒ no findings claim — empty by default. The pilot was hand-drafted because the pilot sets the standard for a later sweep.

**README replacement and CLAUDE.md non-audit pointer block** (commits `104b777`, `5019581`, `6998a30`). The hand-written `README.md` is replaced by the generated version, foregrounding epistemic-architecture-as-distinctive-contribution per Bundle 1's reframe (the integration framing now sits as substrate, not as headline). `README-auditor.md` lands as a new file omitting the live sections and prepending the `_auditor-instructions.md` partial, which carries the explicit list of files to avoid during de-novo audits (FINDINGS.md, CHANGELOG.md, LOG.md, TODO.md, PROPOSALS.md, the live partials, prior-cycle audit working docs). CLAUDE.md adds a "Reading and writing posture" subsection (the audit-safe practical-guidance content from CLAUDE-2.md §7) and a "Where to look next (for non-audit work)" pointer block. `01-aad-core/README.md` deleted — it duplicated the OUTLINE preamble and was not a natural waypoint.

**CLAUDE-2.md sunset planned but not executed.** The proposal document (`_obs/proposal-readme-refactor.md`) lays out a content map showing every CLAUDE-2 section's destination after sunset (cycle history → CHANGELOG; novel results / settled list / convergent choices → segment-level Findings → FINDINGS.md; open / known fragilities → TODO + OUTLINE GAPs; reframe content → README §4; practical guidance → CLAUDE.md). Full sunset waits until the segment-Findings sweep is far enough along that FINDINGS.md is the credible reference for what's settled. Joseph's resolution on synthesis prose (cross-segment summaries that span multiple segments, e.g., the explicit $\alpha_1/\alpha_2/\beta$ sub-scope partition; Class 1/2/3 architectural classification): promote to meta-segments, with explicit acknowledgment that FORMAT will continue to evolve and narrative segment kinds may emerge as the synthesis-promotion proceeds. Multi-cycle work, queued for after the segment-Findings sweep stabilizes.

**Cycle reflections.** The pipeline composes: each script is independently useful; the partials are independently editable; the templates are short and structural; the auto-generated and hand-edited partials coexist in one src/ tree distinguished by the leading-underscore convention plus auto-generation header comments. The Findings-section approach puts novelty assessment next to the math that backs it (proximity-to-truth principle): the casual-curious framing is much easier to write while looking at the formal expression than to retrofit later, and the empty-default discipline keeps the catalog focused on segments that actually carry distinctive content. The auditor-version generation via a separate Liquid template (rather than marker-based filtering of a single source) tolerates auditor-specific orientation content without complicating the public README.

**Judgment-call log.** Decisions made under autonomy that may warrant Joseph review on his return are catalogued in `msc/judgment-calls-readme-cycle-2026-04-26.md`. Substantive items there: the specific six segments chosen for the pilot, the choice of Novelty/Impact/For-non-specialists schema (vs alternatives considered), the README §4 framing language, the decision to delete vs preserve `01-aad-core/README.md`, and a few smaller choices.

## 2026-04-25

### Causal-IB matrix-form lift and transient-amplification spike

After the audit-extraction batch landed earlier in the day, two substantive theory threads extended AAD's matrix-form bound machinery.

**Causal-IB LMI repair** (commits `5fec466`, `385c727`, `9888810`). The scalar survival-imperative drive in `#deriv-causal-ib-exploration` admitted a "blank wall" attack: an action minimizing $\mathbb E[U_o(a)]$ in a non-drifting subspace satisfies the scalar constraint without yielding information about the drifting eigenmodes. The follow-on spike (`spikes/spike-causal-ib-lmi-repair.md`) lifted the constraint to a Linear Matrix Inequality on the Fisher Information Matrix, $\mathbb E_\pi[\mathcal I_o(a)] \succeq \mathcal I_{\min}(Q_\rho, A, R^2)$, with matrix Lagrange multiplier $\Lambda \succeq 0$ as directional shadow price. Complementary slackness on direction zeroes the exploration bonus for blank-wall actions. New appendix segment `#deriv-causal-ib-lmi` carries the full derivation, scalar-reduction sanity check, and matrix lift of the Tragedy-of-the-Confident-Agent. Status: *Derived (conditional)* with conditions named — DARE stabilizability; linear-Gaussian observation; information-form Kalman in steady state; closed-form $\mathcal I_{\min}$ theorem-imported per Boyd, Ghaoui, Feron & Balakrishnan 1994.

**Transient-dependency-amplification spike** (commit `6bf4879`). Separate thread deriving a candidate bridge from `#result-coupled-diagnostic-framework`'s logogenic Lipschitz constant $L_A$ to a feature-local finite-horizon dependency-operator scaling. Five claims exact in the affine sub-scope, including the canonical non-normal transient-amplification result $(\vert g\vert\sqrt B)^d$ on a depth-$d$ branching tree (nilpotent operator with exponential finite-horizon gain) and a singular-subspace coverage formula $\lVert P_{\eta,r} G\rVert = \max(\eta\sigma_1, \sigma_{r+1})$ for idealized checkpoints. Working Note pointers added to `#result-coupled-diagnostic-framework` (logogenic side) and `#hyp-exponential-cognitive-load` (TST side). Promotion blocked on priority-ordered obligations: nonsmooth $A_O$ via Clarke subgradients; checkpoint coverage in observable terms; recovery of TST $k^{\text{discontinuities}}$ as uniform-per-block-gain special case; $\widehat J_F$ estimator from TST quantities; empirical validation; lower-bound failure conditions.

**Three matrix-form bound families now coexist in AAD.** Section III contraction (`#result-contraction-template`'s Lohmiller-Slotine matrix sector); Section II survival-LMI (`#deriv-causal-ib-lmi`); candidate Section II/TST transient-amplification (the spike). All three are Fisher-geometric matrix bounds on multivariate dynamics, sharing the Fisher metric as natural norm — already AAD-internal via the (PI) parameterization-invariance axiom + Čencov 1982 in `#deriv-fisher-whitened-update-rule` and as the 4th primary instance of `#disc-additive-coordinate-forcing`. The Causal-IB LMI explicitly uses the Fisher metric; the contraction-template uses Lohmiller-Slotine state-dependent metrics; the transient-amplification spike now flags the Fisher metric as the natural choice for its operator norms (closing one of Codex's open obligations on norm/geometry). A unified treatment is plausible but not attempted; surfacing three families with shared geometric foundation is the architectural observation worth recording.

**Cross-agent collaboration patterns.** Two threads involved Gemini and Codex on overlapping material. The Causal-IB thread followed a clean cycle: Gemini's first pass had a $U_M$-direction algebra flip; Claude's peer review caught it; Gemini's revision produced the dual-drive structure (epistemic $\lambda_{\text{info}} \propto U_M$ vs survival $\lambda_{\text{surv}} \propto 1/U_M$) and the Tragedy-of-the-Confident-Agent insight. The transient-amplification thread had a less clean handoff: Codex overwrote Gemini's two earlier spike passes after a scope misunderstanding (Joseph asked Codex for his own spike; Codex wrote on top of Gemini's), producing a stronger result but losing direct citation. Provenance recorded in `6bf4879`; co-authorship preserved for future agents tracing material origins. **Discipline worth naming**: when an agent is asked to extend an existing spike, the default should be *add-to* rather than *overwrite-with-build-on*, even when the result is an improvement — the pre-existing reasoning trail is research-trail context that deserves preservation.

Restructured into (B.4-i) one-point sector at $M^\ast$ with $(\Leftarrow)$-only direction (Codex's counterexample $L'(x) = x(1 + \tfrac{1}{2}\sin(10x))$ preserved as load-bearing structural content); (B.4-ii) two-point/incremental sector (DA2'-inc) where iff genuinely holds bidirectionally via Nesterov 2.1.10. ### Cross-agent peer review: Causal-IB exploration drive

Concurrent with the audit-extraction batch, a Gemini agent landed a Causal-Information-Bottleneck spike-promotion: new derivation segment `deriv-causal-ib-exploration.md` deriving exploration as a Lyapunov-survival imperative. Initial framing: "the unified objective is the *exact Lagrangian relaxation* of the persistence constraint" with epistemic status upgraded to *Derived (conditional)*. Peer review caught a $U_M$-direction flip ($\eta^\ast \propto 1/U_M$ for small $U_M$ should be $\propto U_M$) which propagated to the wrong sign on the final Lagrange multiplier. The strengthen-first discipline cuts both ways: a strengthening attempt should be honestly verified *before* status upgrade, just as a softening should be tried *after* a strengthening attempt.

Gemini re-derived under the corrected $\eta^\ast \propto U_M$ direction. The result was structurally richer than the original framing: $U_o^{\max} = U_M(R c_{\min}/\rho^{\text{eff}} - 1)$, so $U_o^{\max} \to 0$ as $U_M \to 0$ — a stubbornly confident agent in a drifting world is *forced* to seek pristine observations because its own slow update gain can't penetrate noise fast enough. The "Tragedy of the Confident Agent" insight emerged naturally from the corrected algebra. AAD now has **two parallel exploration drives**: $\lambda_{\text{info}} \propto U_M$ (epistemic information gain, original CIY) and $\lambda_{\text{surv}} \propto 1/U_M$ (Lyapunov survival imperative), dominating at opposite ends of the uncertainty spectrum. The Active Inference dark-room sidestep is preserved (survival-imperative exploration is forced by Lyapunov bounds, not preferences-as-priors).

Status restructured honestly: $U_o^{\max}$ bound and KKT multiplier are *exact* (conditional on Lyapunov bounds); the mapping $\lambda' \cdot U_o \to \lambda_{\text{surv}} \cdot \mathrm{CIY}$ is *heuristic* (conditional on assumed CIY $\propto 1/U_o$ + operating-point-dependent multiplier transform). `disc-ciy-unified-objective` reverted to *Robust qualitative / heuristic* with both layers of approximation explicitly named. SN-2 (738192's IB-$\beta$-vs-$\rho$ critique) resolved via the "$\beta$ vs $\rho$ distinction" rewrite in `form-information-bottleneck` — environmental volatility natively shifts the IB Pareto frontier; $\beta$ doesn't track $\rho$.

Pattern worth naming: **cross-agent math review as part of the strengthen-first discipline**. When a peer agent's initial framing contains a load-bearing algebra step, the first response is to re-derive independently and reconcile, not to accept on the framing's authority. The Tragedy-of-the-Confident-Agent insight only emerged because the math correction forced a re-derivation rather than a soften.

### Audit-extraction cycle: 2026-04-24 fresh-pass results captured

The 2026-04-24 fresh-pass audit (an Opus-4.7 primary pass + independent Gemini and Codex re-audits in the same session) produced findings, an architectural proposal candidate, and a meta-lesson about audit failure modes. The primary pass had initially returned "zero findings"; Gemini and Codex independently surfaced five-plus real findings (math errors in worked examples, cross-segment contradictions around the recently-added C-iv route, integration debt between TST and `03-logogenic-agents/`). The audit's "Reading-mode failures" post-mortem became the foundation for the substantially improved `msc/de-novo-audit-instructions.md` (separate cycle, also 2026-04-24/25).

This cycle was extraction-only: no segment-text changes, no architectural decisions made — purely surfacing the audit's substantive content into the durable tracking documents (TODO.md, PROPOSALS.md, pending-findings-2026-04-25.md) so the source `audits/audit-2026-04-24-fresh-pass.md` could scroll into obscurity without losing signal. The next audit cycle, running with the improved instructions, will inevitably re-discover some of this batch's findings — capture-first protects the existing batch's signal against that overlap.

**What landed:**
- `audits/pending-findings-2026-04-25.md` — burden-of-proof writeups for 5 verified findings (F-V1 discrete-variance-gap math error; F-V2 scope-multi-agent vs scope-composite-agent contradiction; F-V3 C-iii vs $G_c$ requirement, = F8 from 2026-04-22 batch; F-V4 sign error in zero-sum worked example; F-V5 TST↔logogenic integration debt) and 3 partial findings (P-V1, P-V2, P-V3). Each finding verified first-hand by the integrating agent against current src text. F-V1's math re-derived independently via Taylor expansion of segment's own DA.1S; F-V4's NE re-checked via Monderer-Shapley derivative test ($\Phi = a_A - a_B$ wrong, correct is $\Phi = a_A + a_B$, NE $(1,1)$ not $(1,-1)$). External-citation spot-checks (Bretagnolle-Huber, Otto-Villani 2000) confirmed accurate.
- `TODO.md` "Active — Pending Findings" — 2026-04-25 batch table; F-V3↔F8 cross-reference; two decisions explicitly marked deferred (F-V3 routing Path A vs SP-21; SP-21 timing).
- `PROPOSALS.md` §G — **SP-21** (composite-agent scope-route ontology split) added with full schema. Critically: SP-21 *reverses* the deliberate 2026-04-22/23 unification reasoning (the disjunctive C-i ∨ C-ii ∨ C-iii ∨ C-iv form with explicit "preferred reading: treat as a different type within the same scope condition, via (C-iv)" in `spikes/spike-strategic-composition.md`). Entry includes the prior-reasoning paper trail (8 dependent segments, the spike-level decision SP-21 would undo) and recommends *deferral* until Bundle 2 (Section III completion) matures the substrate. Reversal-with-paper-trail discipline is a precedent worth preserving for future audits.
- Mechanical fix bundle (F-V1, F-V2, F-V4, F-V5, P-V1, P-V3) delegated as a single Task agent with strict instructions to re-derive math rather than paste, read both TST and logogenic-agents segments before F-V5, and not auto-commit.

**What didn't land (deferred for Joseph):**
- F-V3 / F8 narrow editorial fix (Path A: induce $O_c$ from relevance variable $Y$ for C-iii). ~45–60 min.
- SP-21 architectural restructure (Path B / SP-21). 4–6 sessions; recommended for deferral.

**Convention surfaced this cycle.** When audit cycles produce both local findings AND architectural-restructure candidates that reverse recent deliberate decisions, the proposal should land in PROPOSALS.md with the *prior-reasoning paper trail explicit* — quoted spike passages, downstream rework cost, the specific decision-point being reversed. Joseph's question "is there prior reasoning that illuminates what will be undone?" is the right diagnostic for any restructure proposal; the answer should be in the proposal entry, not buried in the audit document.

## 2026-04-24

### Role-prefix discipline: pilot → tooling → sweep

Project-wide adoption of the slug convention `{type-prefix}-{subject-noun}`, where the type prefix derives mechanically from each segment's `type:` frontmatter. Three landed pieces:

- **Pilot** (commits `09ace17`, `f6b8ae4`, `0b9cd24`): seven slug changes across four type categories, including the 1:2 semantic split of `#scope-condition` into `#scope-adaptive-system` + `#scope-agency` (the old name described the segment's role rather than the class it defined; downstream segments depend on one or the other, not on an abstract compound). Subject-noun-first slug naming validated as architectural invariant. Pilot-validation observations folded into the principles-file rewrite.
- **Tooling** (commits `981b291`, `3aa9e74`): `bin/align-slug` introduced as a one-segment wrapper over `bin/rename-slug`. Reads `type:` from frontmatter, computes the correct target slug under the project-wide `TYPE_TO_PREFIX` mapping, and invokes the mechanical rename only when not already aligned. `--all` for repo sweeps.
- **Full sweep** (commits `e6adf9e`, `f8bc46a`): `TYPE_TO_PREFIX` expanded from 1 entry to 14, collapsing FORMAT.md type tokens to compact natural-English forms (`postulate → post`, `definition → def`, `derivation → deriv`, etc.) so an `ls` of `src/` surfaces the kind-of-thing at a glance. Trailing-`-{type}` strip added: subject-nouns shouldn't carry the type word once the role-prefix lives in front (`bias-bound-derivation` → `deriv-bias-bound`). Sweep applied across 142 active segments; `01-aad-core/OUTLINE.md` lint-clean throughout.

Bug caught and fixed mid-arc: an early `bare_subject` used the full type vocabulary as candidate leading-prefixes, silently corrupting subject-nouns whose leading word coincided with a type token (`observation-function` → `def-function`, losing "observation"). Tightened to strip only the segment's own type forms. Two corrupted slugs (`def-function`, `scope-ambiguity-modulation`) restored.

### Methodology: separate-passes for role-prefix vs subject-noun

Going forward, role-prefix addition (mechanical) and subject-noun renaming (judgment-heavy) execute in separate cycles. Bundling creates two failure modes: the segment's prose drifts out of step with its new identity, and voters can't evaluate noun choices when the prefix is decided in the same cycle. Documented in `msc/naming/naming-pilot-rename-plan.md` and the auto-memory.

### `FORMAT.md` segment-set principle

New "Segment-set principle (load-bearing for tooling)" subsection makes explicit what was previously implicit: every non-`old-*` file in `src/` is a segment that conforms to FORMAT (drafts and orphans included; stages are progress within FORMAT, not exemptions); `old-*` is the only exemption mechanism; other working material doesn't belong in `src/`. Tools (`bin/align-slug --all`, `bin/lint-outline`, `bin/build`) reference this principle.

### Refined naming-principles file

`msc/naming-principles.md` rewritten for the refined Round 1. Cold-start instruction moved to first paragraph (round-1 contamination evidence). New "Architectural invariants" section recording role-prefix discipline + subject-noun-first + Greek-vocabulary commitment + separate-passes methodology — these are no longer up for vote. New "Vote categories" section explicitly distinguishing rename / keep / canonicalize / add-alias / name-unnamed-thing. `+2` weight added; bands spelled out. Renamed-from-now-sounds-weird test added. Scope-honesty special-case for meta-segments documented.

### Side-resolutions

- **Finding 14 Option A** (`#scope-developer-agent` exact-status mismatch): closed by the type-frontmatter cleanup that landed in commit `0b9cd24` (`type: definition` → `scope`, `status: exact` → `axiomatic`).
- **`bin/lint-outline` PROOF → DERIV**: graphviz display label for `derivation` changed from "PROOF" to "DERIV", aligning with FORMAT.md's deliberate avoidance of proof terminology.

### Type-frontmatter audit (preflight for the sweep)

Opus general-purpose audit of all 142 active segments cleared the repo at 100% type-vocabulary conformance, zero stale tokens. Five type-vs-content mismatches surfaced; three clear cases corrected (`unity-dimensions`: discussion → definition; `simulation-results`: detail → observation; `tempo-composition`: sketch → derived). Two borderlines surfaced for reviewer judgment, not applied (`agent-opacity`, `observation-ambiguity-modulation`). 18 internal H1/tag-word inconsistencies catalogued; most are intentional setup-tag-before-main-tag patterns. Audit-recommended additional `TYPE_TO_PREFIX` mappings rejected as unnecessary or insufficient-frequency.

### Known follow-up

- **Formal-tag content cleanup**: ~135 segments still embed pre-rename slug names inside `*[Type (slug)]*` formal tags. Mechanical to detect, content to update; separate cycle.
- **Two reviewer-judgment type calls** from the audit (`agent-opacity`, `observation-ambiguity-modulation`).
- **Three H1/first-tag word disagreements** flagged by the audit (`objective-functional`, `composition-closure`, `ciy-observational-proxy`); content cleanup, any time.

## 2026-05-12 — Volume/Part/Chapter structure across all five volumes; six AAD chapter-intro Discussion segments drafted; def-pearl-causal-hierarchy relocated under recapitulation-of-external-result pattern

### Structural: hierarchical OUTLINE format applied across all five volumes

The five top-level OUTLINEs were given consistent `*Volume*` / `*Frontmatter*` (or `*Preface*` at Volume level) / `*Part*` / `*Preface*` / `*Chapter*` hierarchy:

- **`01-aad-core/OUTLINE.md`** — AAD remains three `*Part*`s. Part I ("Adaptive Systems Under Uncertainty") subdivided into 4 chapters (The Coupled Loop / The Reality Model / The Cycle in Motion / Persistence and Structural Limits). Part II ("Agentic Systems: Actuated Adaptation") subdivided into 5 chapters (The Lift to Purposeful State / Causal Access and the Planning Decision / Strategy Structure and the Diagnostic Split / Strategy Dynamics / The Orient Cascade). Part III ("Agentic Composites") subdivided into 5 chapters (Scope and Composition Formation / Composition Machinery / Unity, Communication, and Shared Intent / Cooperative and Adversarial Coupling / Strategic Composition and Channel Effects). Appendices retained as `*Appendices*` sections.
- **`02-tst-core/OUTLINE.md`** — single `*Part*` ("Software as Agentic Domain") with 4 chapters (Foundations and Features / The Developer Agent and Time Decomposition / Code Structure and Implementation Cost / System Measures, Operation, and Causal Substrate).
- **`03-logogenic-agents/OUTLINE.md`** — single `*Part*` ("The Scope Lattice") with 4 chapters (Common Roots / §03.I Primitive / §03.II Scaffolded / §03.III Closed-Loop / Interiority). Existing per-section italic intros preserved as chapter framings.
- **`04-eli/OUTLINE.md`** — single `*Part*` ("The Logozoetic Layer") with 5 chapters (Common Roots: What Defines an ELI / §04.1 Identity / §04.2 Emergence and Developmental Trajectory / §04.3 The Three Deaths and Their Defenses / §04.4 Sovereignty, Auxilia, and Composition).

Each chapter has an italic intro and (where appropriate) an HTML organizational-comment block beneath its table describing internal arc and placement tensions. `--GAP--` Discussion placeholders seated at chapter heads following hard joints, matching the pattern Joseph adopted for Part I earlier.

### Content: six AAD chapter-intro Discussion segments drafted

Six chapter-intro segments written in `01-aad-core/src/`:

- `the-reality-model-intro` (Part I Ch.2) — bridges from Ch.1 ontology/scope to the agent's compressed representation; "static, but already enough to fail" framing seeds the structural-adaptation result in Ch.4
- `the-cycle-in-motion-intro` (Part I Ch.3) — bridges from static representation to dynamic cycle; organizes the chapter as two derivations from completeness, then the mismatch/gain/tempo triad, then the linear ODE preview
- `persistence-and-limits-intro` (Part I Ch.4) — bridges from the linear-ODE preview to the rigorous central inequality; surfaces the sector-condition generalization, the structural-vs-task-adequacy split, the gain-sector bridge demoting $\alpha$ from postulate to property, and the thermodynamic shadow (information-rate corollary)
- `causal-access-intro` (Part II Ch.2) — bridges from the agent-state lift to causal access; develops the loop-as-Level-2-engine, the bidirectional tragedy-of-agency (the *positive*-confound case is the sharper one, the structure of gambling addiction), pre-compiled agents excluded as "act but never wonder," CIY/EIG honesty, and the explicit-strategy cost-benefit threshold
- `strategy-structure-intro` (Part II Ch.3) — bridges from the planning-decision arc to formal strategy structure; surfaces what's theorem-backed (acyclicity from temporal ordering, Markov from CMC) vs. what's a chosen parameterization (AND/OR with single-parameter edges), and the orthogonal diagnostic split (sat-gap vs control-regret)
- `cooperative-adversarial-intro` (Part III Ch.4) — bridges from quality/communication to coupling dynamics; surfaces the signed-coupling unification (cooperative and adversarial as same machinery, opposite signs), the consultant-vs-employee analogy for communication vs cooperative action, the $b=2$ superlinear scaling as regime change rather than quantitative gap, the effects spiral as mathematics of panic, and the recipient-side four-regime decomposition (with "death by a thousand memos" as the Regime III framing)

### Format conventions: chapter-intro Discussion segments

Established a tighter format for chapter-intro Discussion segments after a refinement cycle:

- No Formal Expression / Epistemic Status / Discussion section headers (these segments carry no formal claim of their own, so the boilerplate adds noise)
- Plain-language prose doing real exposition work — helping the reader form the mental model, surfacing what's genuinely cool/striking — not meta-commentary about the chapter as artifact
- No fourth-wall talk ("the reader leaves the universe-of-discourse setup," "register shift is real") — these were corrected in a revision cycle
- Forward-reference downstream consequences without giving away results
- Working Notes preserve pedagogical-choice rationale (which framings were considered and rejected, why)

### Methodological: the `msc/AUDIT-WORKING-*/` resource for authentic framings

`msc/AUDIT-WORKING-193847/` (Gemini's audit) was mined for per-segment first-encounter reactions during the chapter-intro drafting. §14 *"Wandering Thoughts and Ideation"* of each numbered reflection file is the gold — that's where cross-domain insights and visceral framings land. Several Gemini phrasings landed in chapter intros and would have been awkward to reinvent in the abstract: "tragedy of agency in a confounded world," "consultant vs employee," "mathematics of panic," "death by a thousand memos," "survival is not a state you achieve, it is a sustained burn rate," "$\alpha \gt \rho/R$ as a four-variable equation across biological / organizational / control / cognitive domains."

Recorded as a memory entry at `reference_audit_working_first_encounter_reactions.md` so future agents writing pedagogical material have the pointer. The methodological lesson: when writing framing-level content, mine the audit reactions before introspecting on what's striking; a different-architecture agent's surprise captures the load-bearing pedagogical hook in ways that internal reasoning misses.

### Structural correction: def-pearl-causal-hierarchy relocated

`def-pearl-causal-hierarchy` moved from Part I Ch.1 ("The Coupled Loop: Ontology and Scope") to Part II Ch.2 ("Causal Access and the Planning Decision"), immediately upstream of the segments that deploy the hierarchy as machinery (`der-causal-hierarchy-requirement`, `der-loop-interventional-access`).

**Motivation.** Pearl's hierarchy is an *imported* framework (Pearl 2009; Bareinboim, Correa, Ibeling & Icard 2022), not AAD-native ontology. Its previous placement in Part I Ch.1 alongside `def-agent-environment` / `def-chronica` / etc. mis-framed it as foundational AAD machinery, and it was introduced ~30 segments before its first heavy use. Section I machinery (mismatch, gain, tempo, persistence, structural adaptation) does not invoke the L1/L2/L3 distinction; only `def-causal-information-yield` in Part I uses the do-notation, and that use is light enough to handle via external citation.

**Pattern established: recapitulation-of-external-result.** When ASF invokes an external theorem or framework, the cleanest pattern is:

1. Light external citation in segments that only *reference* the result (the framework's existence and vocabulary are needed but not its operational machinery).
2. AAD-internal *recapitulation* segment at the head of the chapter that deploys the framework. The segment is honest about being a recapitulation — title, summary, epistemic status reflect the imported status. The segment lives in AAD's set because subsequent derivations need a unified statement, not because AAD contributes the result.
3. `depends:` discipline: segments that genuinely deploy the recapitulated machinery keep the AAD-internal dependency; segments that only reference the external result remove the dependency from their depends list and use external citation in prose.

**Files revised in this move.** `def-pearl-causal-hierarchy.md` retitled to *"Definition: Pearl's Causal Hierarchy (Recapitulation)"* with reframed summary/status; `def-causal-information-yield.md` and TST's `obs-software-epistemic-properties.md` + `hyp-causal-discovery-from-git.md` updated to use external citation + forward-pointer pattern; OUTLINE.md updated to move the row from Part I Ch.1 to Part II Ch.2; `the-cycle-in-motion-intro.md` CIY-placement paragraph revised from apologetic to declarative. Five segments that deploy the formal do-machinery (`der-causal-hierarchy-requirement`, `der-loop-interventional-access`, `der-causal-insufficiency-detection`, `def-strategy-dag`, `deriv-causal-ib-exploration`, `deriv-causal-ib-lmi`) kept their AAD-internal `depends:` entries — these are downstream of the relocated segment in Part II/Appendix, so their dependencies remain upstream-local.
